Remarks.&mdash;During January the highest reading of the barometer was 30.496, on the 17th; and the lowest 30, on the 13th. The highest temperature was 76&deg;, on the 8th; and the lowest 28&deg;, on the 15th and 16th. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded oil the 13th, averaging 15.7 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 10 days, measuring 0.97 inch. During February the highest reading of the barometer was 30.470, on the 28th; and the lowest 30, on the 18th. The highest temperature was 740, on the 8th; and the lowest 40&deg;, on the 18th. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 17th, averaging 13.9 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 10 days, measuring 0.23 inch. During March the highest reading of the barometer was 30.420, on the 6tli; and the lowest 29.912, on the 18th. The highest temperature was 8i&deg;, on the 31st; and the lowest 450, on the 1st. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 23rd, averaging 12.1 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 18 days, measuring 3.37 inches. IX. * w 1893.] LAPPA. 201 (Received 17 th April 1893.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, 12th April 1893. SIR, I.&mdash;a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Secdnd Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the first quarter of the year 1893, January-March. b. The Custom House m a; H&amp;gt; was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Tk 30 and Hk.Tk 80 per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com&not; menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House &#39; Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow (.1 w &raquo; and Chienshan m iii) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (G ft), Kuttai (Hf 5S), and Kwanchiap (&sect;fj pgj) sub-stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
